This framework protects the firm's capital while maintaining exposure to the bull thesis if it validates. It's not aggressive. It's not cautious. It's disciplined.

Execute this. Either the thesis validates at earnings and you deploy bulk capital with full conviction, or it doesn't and you've protected capital that would have been lost on false assumptions.

That's how you actually beat the market—not through bigger convictions, but through better risk management that forces management and the market to prove their case before you commit.

You could measure revenue per data center, measure gross margin per customer cohort, and see that the ROI was working. Alphabet hasn't given us that visibility. They're bundling capex ROI into forward guidance. That's opacity, and opacity is expensive in a rising-rate environment.

That's not a minor variation. That's an inversion of your bull case.

At that point, you've waited for confirmation that never materialized, and you've now missed 7-10% of the move you were trying to avoid buying at the top of.

Your premise—that waiting for a break below $325.98 will give you "clarity"—assumes that confirmation must come in the form of a deeper breakdown. But confirmation can also come in the form of fundamental data that validates the bull case. You're weighting the technical confirmation much more heavily than the fundamental data flow.

Here's what really concerns me about your recommendation: you're treating the staged entry plan like a failure if it doesn't get triggered. You frame waiting for weakness as prudent, but what you're actually saying is "I'd rather miss 10% of upside than risk 10% of downside." In a market where fundamentals support $400-450 and the balance sheet can support capex intensity indefinitely, that's actually not a favorable trade-off over a 24-month horizon.

THE NEUTRAL RISK APPROACH: TACTICAL AGILITY OVER BINARY CONVICTION¶

Phase One (Execute Now): Deploy 30% of target allocation at $355.

  1. You capture Scenario A (60% probability) if it unfolds. The stock consolidates and drifts higher, and you've got a core position that participated.

  2. You don't get blown out on a sudden rally. If $355 is a genuine support level and the stock bounces to $380-390 over the next 6 weeks without ever pulling back to $340, you're already in and you participated in that 7-10% move. Waiting for a pullback that never comes is wealth destruction.

Don't say "I'll add at $340 and $320." Say this instead:

  • If the stock pulls back below the 200-SMA at $325.98, that's a meaningful technical breakdown. Deploy the remaining 20-25% there, because below that level you're testing genuine support that would suggest either a bottoming process or something fundamentally wrong.

The difference between this and the staged entry plan is subtle but critical: you're not assuming where the pullback will come. You're creating decision trees based on either fundamental validation OR technical breakdown, and you're sizing differently depending on which one occurs first.

Against the Aggressive Case:

That's optionality.

Against the Conservative Case:

You're not sitting in 100% cash hoping for perfect entry conditions that don't materialize.

THE CRITICAL INSIGHT BOTH SIDES MISSED:¶

The real asymmetry isn't "10% downside vs 25% upside." The real asymmetry is "managing the range of uncertainty better than a binary position."

The aggressive analyst assumes you can time the stages perfectly and add discipline when your position is underwater. That's hard.

The conservative analyst assumes the breakdown will come in a way that lets you enter better. That's equally hard.

The middle ground—deploy some capital now to capture the optionality of higher prices, keep most dry to take advantage of either fundamental clarity OR technical breakdown—is actually easier to execute because it doesn't require perfect timing or perfect discipline. It requires clear decision trees and the willingness to adjust sizing based on what the market actually shows you, not what you predicted it would show.

Rating: Overweight

Current Price: $356.13
